Live app · UI audit · 2026-10-21

74 confirmed UI issues, with the reasoning

The live app at goldito-petcare.vercel.app (main) checked against the README, DESIGN.md, the Figma file and the design site's Draft: visual fidelity, components, copy, motion and microinteractions, accessibility, states, README claims, Draft divergence and the desktop frame. Ten finders worked one dimension each; every finding then faced three independent checks (reproduce it, read the rule it cites, weigh its impact) and only survivors are listed. Duplicates across finders were merged by hand after the merge agent dropped on a network error. 0 were refuted (listed at the bottom with the reason), and 65 lower-priority items were left out by the finders' caps.

F52

Placeholder text is drawn at 50 percent opacity and lands at 2.2:1 on white

highAccessibilityFrontend (us)effort S

Chat message box 'Message Chloe', every TextField placeholder (pet form, profile, login, signup, place sheet), owner onboarding BigInput · /chat/[threadId], /owner/pets/new, /owner/pets/[petId], /profile, /login, /signup, /owner-onboarding · both

Actual

placeholderTextColor is `${textMuted}80` (hex alpha 50 percent). Live computed ::placeholder color rgba(107, 93, 74, 0.5) over the white input = #B5AEA5, contrast 2.2:1 at 16 px.

Expected

Placeholder text at 4.5:1 (WCAG 1.4.3 applies to placeholder text). DESIGN.md §9: no opacity tints for text. Figma Text Field: placeholder is theme.color.textMuted (6.4:1 on white).

WCAG 1.4.3; DESIGN.md §9 (No opacity tints for text or its background) and §6.1 TextField; Figma Text Field Figma 1727:82029 (figma/components.md line 24: placeholder theme.color.textMuted)

Suggested fix

Use theme.color.textMuted with no alpha in TextField.tsx:23, app/chat/[threadId].tsx:647 and ownerOnboarding/BigInput.tsx:12. If Mook wants a lighter hint, add a solid placeholder token to tokens.ts that passes 4.5:1 on surface and background, and add it to the Figma Text Field as a variable.

Why we're confident (three independent checks)
ReproducedCode confirmed: TextField.tsx:23, app/chat/[threadId].tsx:647 and ownerOnboarding/BigInput.tsx:12 all pass placeholderTextColor `${theme.color.textMuted}80` (hex alpha 0x80 = 50 percent). Live results.json chat.textarea: placeholderColor rgba(107, 93, 74, 0.5) on #ffffff, composited #b5aea5, contrast 2.2 at 16 px.TextField.tsx:22-23 '// A hint, not a value: lighter than typed text. placeholderTextColor={`${theme.color.textMuted}80`}'; results.json chat.textarea placeholderContrast 2.2, placeholderOverBg '#b5aea5'. PR #215 grows the message box to 4 lines and adds the AI draft label but, from its described scope, does not change placeholderTextColor, so this is not handled there. The TextField comment shows the alpha was a deliberate 'hint lighter than typed text' choice; the fix should keep that intent with a solid token.Severity high; owner frontend; effort S. Textmuted #6B5D4A on white is about 6.4:1, which still reads lighter than text #1B4332.

F90

On short desktop windows the phone's tab bar and primary action sit below the fold and the wheel over the phone cannot reveal them

highDesktopFrontend (us)effort S

Desktop phone frame, every screen; the bottom tab bar, Add pet and the Show both phones link · /owner (any route in the frame) · desktop

Actual

DeviceFrame clamps the screen to a minimum of 600 px and scales only by width, so the frame always needs 724 px of height (28 label + 624 phone + 40 footer + 32 margins). At 1093x614 (a 1366x768 Windows laptop at 125 percent), 960x600 (1440x900 at 150 percent or browser zoom 150 percent) and 1366x650 (a 768 px screen minus browser chrome and taskbar) the phone body ends at 668 px: the tab bar, the home indicator and the Show both phones link are cut off. The inner app's html has overscroll-behavior: contain, so a wheel over the phone scrolls the app 22 px and the outer page 0 px; the outer page only scrolls when the pointer is over the grey backdrop (110 px at 1093x614, 124 px at 960x600). At 1024x700 the link is clipped too (page 724 px in a 700 px window).

Expected

DESIGN.md 7.7 desktop check for every screen at 1366x768: the primary action is visible, nothing is cut off at the bottom, wheel scrolling works. Phase 10.9 DoD: frame not clipped at 1366x768, 1440x900, 1920x1080 and Windows 125 and 150 percent scaling. DESIGN.md 2.1: short windows shrink only the screen height, but the whole phone must still fit.

Rule or reference

DESIGN.md 7.7 (desktop check list, 1366x768), DESIGN.md 2.1 (short windows, min 600), docs/plan/phases/phase-10-deploy.md task 10.9 DoD

Evidence

desktop-verify/fit-1093x614.png, fit-960x600.png, fit-1366x650.png, fit-1024x700.png and desktop-verify/results.json fit entries (phoneBody.bottom 668, link.bottom 697, afterPhoneWheel 0, afterBackdropWheel 110/124/74); live/desktop__frame-1024.png (link clipped at the bottom edge); frontend/components/shell/DeviceFrame.web.tsx:10 (MIN_SCREEN_HEIGHT = 600), :36 (scale from width only), :38-41 (height clamp); frontend/components/shell/TouchEmulation.web.ts:53 (html, body overscroll-behavior: contain)

live · frame-1024

Suggested fix

In DeviceFrame.web.tsx compute scale = min(widthScale, (windowHeight - 2*PAGE_MARGIN - extras) / (MIN_SCREEN_HEIGHT + 2*BEZEL)) clamped to MIN_SCALE, so a 600 px screen always fits by shrinking the phone; keep the footer link inside the fitted height. Add frame.spec.ts cases at 1093x614 and 960x600 asserting the tab bar and the Show both phones link are inside the viewport. The overscroll change is optional.

Why we're confident (three independent checks)
ReproducedThe geometry is real: results.json fit entries for 1093x614, 960x600 and 1366x650 all report phoneBody.bottom 668, link.bottom 697 with visible false, afterPhoneWheel 0 and afterBackdropWheel 110, 124 and 74. fit-1093x614.png shows the tab bar labels touching the bottom edge of the window with the home indicator and the Show both phones link cut off. One imprecision: at 1093x614 the tab bar is still partly visible (icons and labels), only its lower padding, the home indicator and the link are clipped; at 960x600 the labels are also lost. At 1366x768 proper the phone fits (phoneBody.bottom 712, link.bottom 741).desktop-verify/results.json fit[4] (1093x614): phoneBody.bottom 668, link.visible false, innerScrollAfterPhoneWheel 22, afterBackdropWheel 110. DeviceFrame.web.tsx:10 MIN_SCREEN_HEIGHT = 600, :36 scale from windowWidth only, :38-41 Math.max(MIN_SCREEN_HEIGHT, ...) height clamp. TouchEmulation.web.ts:53 'html, body { overscroll-behavior: contain; }'. Corrected wording: at Windows 125 percent (1093x614) the tab bar is clipped at its lower edge and the home indicator and link are off screen; at 150 percent (960x600) the tab labels are cut too.
Rule checkedDESIGN.md 2.1 says the whole phone must fit on short windows ('only the screen height shrinks (min 600)') and 7.7's desktop check requires 'The primary action is visible; nothing is cut off at the bottom'. Phase 10.9's DoD names Windows 125 and 150 percent scaling explicitly. The 7.7 checklist is written for 1366x768, where the frame does fit, so the strongest rule is the 10.9 DoD and 2.1, not 7.7.DESIGN.md 2.1: 'On short windows (laptops at 1366 x 768, Windows at 125-150 % scaling) only the screen height shrinks (min 600).' DESIGN.md 7.7 checklist: 'The primary action is visible; nothing is cut off at the bottom.' phase-10-deploy.md line 67 (10.9 DoD): frames and panel not clipped at 1366x768, 1440x900, 1920x1080 plus Windows 125 and 150 percent.
Impact weighedA judge on a 1366x768 Windows laptop at 125 percent scaling is common, and the browser chrome takes more height still, so a clipped tab bar on every screen is noticeable; at 150 percent the tab labels disappear. High is defensible because it hits every screen for that class of machine and the wheel over the phone cannot recover it. The fix is small and purely frontend; PR #215 does not touch the shell.PR #215 diff has 0 lines under components/shell. DeviceFrame.web.tsx already scales the whole phone for narrow windows (:36), so extending the scale to the height axis is a one-line change plus a test.

F98

mediumAccessibilityFrontend (us)effort S

Browser tab title and history entries for every route, inside and outside the frame · /owner, /owner/bookings, /profile, /settings, /privacy and all others · both

Actual

document.title is Goldito for all ten routes probed, in the outer page and inside the iframe, even though the Tabs and Stack screens declare titles (Home, Bookings, Profile, Settings, Chat). The outer page mirrors the URL into the address bar but not the title, and the inner app never sets one.

Expected

WCAG 2.4.2 Page Titled: each page has a title that describes its topic or purpose; DESIGN.md 9 asks for the web demo to work for keyboard and switch users, who rely on titles when switching tabs. A judge with the owner and sitter in two tabs sees two tabs both named Goldito.

Rule or reference

Suggested fix

Set document.title to '<Screen> · Goldito' from the screen options (a useDocumentTitle hook in RoleTabs.tsx and the Stack layouts, or expo-router/head), and have ParentUrlSync in AppShell.web.tsx also write the iframe's document.title to the parent on each sync; in split view prefix 'Owner phone' or 'Sitter phone' from accountLabel. Add one Playwright check that /owner and /owner/bookings produce different titles.

Why we're confident (three independent checks)
ReproducedReal: every probed route reports document.title Goldito both in the iframe and in the outer page, while the layouts declare per-screen titles and nothing in the app sets document.title.desktop-verify/results.json titles: ten entries (/owner, /owner/bookings, /owner/feed, /owner/diary, /owner/snap, /profile, /settings, /whats-new, /privacy, /this-route-does-not-exist) all with frameTitle 'Goldito' and outerTitle 'Goldito'. frontend/app/owner/(tabs)/_layout.tsx:9-15 sets title Home, Bookings, Feed, Diary, Snap; grep for document.title, <Head or useDocumentTitle across frontend/app and frontend/components returns nothing; AppShell.web.tsx:174-206 ParentUrlSync only calls window.parent.history.replaceState and never copies the title.
Rule checkedWCAG 2.1 SC 2.4.2 (level A) does require a title that describes the page's topic or purpose, and identical titles on every page is the documented failure of that criterion (F25). But DESIGN.md 9 never mentions page titles or WCAG 2.4.2; it only names keyboard and switch users in the focus-ring bullet, so the 'cited through DESIGN.md 9' link is the finder's inference, not a rule in the guide.DESIGN.md 9 bullets cover contrast, touch targets, accessibilityRole/Label, colour alone, 'Focus is visible: 2 px primary outline, 2 px offset, on everything focusable (keyboard and switch users on the web demo)', reduce motion, and image alternatives; no bullet on titles. Corrected wording: 'WCAG 2.1 SC 2.4.2 (level A, failure F25); DESIGN.md 9 sets a keyboard-user baseline for the web demo but has no title rule'.
Impact weighedNot refuted: it is a genuine level A failure and the two-tabs-both-named-Goldito case is exactly how judges will run the owner and sitter demo. I would not call it high though: the demo is a phone-framed single app, the browser default ring still leaves users able to navigate, and no judge rubric scores page titles, so medium (a real accessibility gap that is cheap to close) is the honest rating. The fix is a few lines and belongs to frontend.results.json titles show the outer page mirrors the URL (frameUrl equals the address) but not the title; expo-router ships expo-router/head and React Navigation's documentTitle hook, so a small useDocumentTitle in RoleTabs.tsx and the Stack layouts covers every screen. F58

aria-selected sits on role=button day cells and pet selectors, and tab names carry the icon font glyphs

mediumAccessibilityFrontend (us)effort S

Calendar day buttons (sitter schedule, sitter calendar, request sheet, day picker), Snap pet selector, Mood pet tabs; the bottom tab bar items · /sitter/schedule, /owner/sitters/[sitterId]/calendar, chat request calendar, /owner/snap/mood, /owner/snap/sticker, /owner/snap/treat, every tab screen · both

Actual

Day cells render `button[role=button][aria-selected=false]` (axe aria-allowed-attr, critical, 550 nodes on 30 shots); aria-selected is not permitted on button, so the selected state is not announced. Tab bar items' text content is 'Home' and so on: the Ionicons glyph (private use code points) is in the name twice and nothing is aria-hidden, so some screen readers read a stray character before each tab name.

Expected

Selected state on a valid role (option in a listbox, gridcell in a grid, or aria-pressed on a toggle button); decorative icon glyphs hidden from the accessibility tree so the tab name is just 'Home' (WCAG 4.1.2, 1.3.1).

Rule or reference

WCAG 4.1.2 and 1.3.1; ARIA in HTML (aria-selected not allowed on button); axe aria-allowed-attr (live/summary.json); DESIGN.md §9 roles and labels

Evidence

a11y-verify/results.json calendar.attrs (role button, aria-selected false) and bookings.tabs (codepoints U+F383 x2, hiddenIcon false, ariaLabel null); live/sitter__sitter-schedule.axe.json, live/owner__owner-snap-mood.axe.json, live/owner__state-chat-request-calendar.axe.json; code: frontend/components/SlotCalendar.tsx:210 (`aria-selected={selected}` on a button Pressable), frontend/features/snap/SnapPets.tsx:72, frontend/features/snap/MoodScreen.tsx:240, frontend/components/RoleTabs.tsx:23-40 (TabIcon renders Ionicons with no accessibilityElementsHidden; RoleTabs.tsx:67 aria-selected on role=tab is fine)

Suggested fix

SlotCalendar, DayPickerSheet, RangeCalendar: drop aria-selected and use aria-pressed={selected} on the button cells (or role grid with gridcell). SnapPets and MoodScreen: replace accessibilityState selected plus aria-selected with aria-pressed, or role radio in a radiogroup like the bookings tabs already do. TabIcon: pass accessibilityElementsHidden and importantForAccessibility='no' to the Ionicons. Re-run axe on the schedule and mood captures to confirm zero aria-allowed-attr.

Why we're confident (three independent checks)
ReproducedBoth halves hold: aria-selected on role=button day cells and pet selectors, and tab items whose accessible text contains the icon glyph twice.frontend/components/SlotCalendar.tsx:208 to 210 accessibilityRole button plus aria-selected={selected}; features/snap/SnapPets.tsx:70 to 72 accessibilityRole button, accessibilityState selected, aria-selected; features/snap/MoodScreen.tsx:239 to 240 same. live/sitter__sitter-schedule.axe.json aria-allowed-attr critical, 6 nodes, html button role=button aria-selected=false; live/owner__owner-snap-mood.axe.json same rule, 2 nodes. a11y-verify/results.json bookings.tabs: text 'Home', codepoints U+F383 x2, hiddenIcon false, ariaLabel null. components/RoleTabs.tsx:23 to 40 TabIcon renders Ionicons with no accessibilityElementsHidden; RoleTabs.tsx:64 to 67 uses role tab with aria-selected, which is valid.
Rule checkedARIA in HTML does not allow aria-selected on button; axe flags it as critical; WCAG 4.1.2 requires state to be programmatically exposed on a valid role. DESIGN.md section 9 asks for roles and labels and Figma has nothing that overrides this.axe rule aria-allowed-attr (critical) in the two cited .axe.json files; DESIGN.md:626 to 632 section 9 (roles, labels, non-text contrast of selected states). Note that react-native-web also maps accessibilityState selected to aria-selected, so SnapPets.tsx:71 would still emit it after removing the explicit prop.
Impact weighedCritical-impact axe violations across about 30 shots are the kind of thing an accessibility pass by a judge will surface first, and the fix is a few lines. Medium is right: real WCAG 4.1.2 issue but the visual demo is unaffected. The icon glyph part is lower value (most screen readers skip private-use glyphs) but the fix is one prop.550 aria-allowed-attr nodes on 30 shots per the finding, confirmed in sitter__sitter-schedule.axe.json and owner__owner-snap-mood.axe.json; TabIcon at RoleTabs.tsx:23 to 40.

  • OS text size, browser zoom, and colour scheme (dark mode and the Balanced theme) in the phone frame: DESIGN.md section 9 requires respecting OS text size and section 3.1 defines theme presets (default, Balanced, future pet skins), yet no finder tested text scaling or colour scheme. A grep shows no allowFontScaling or maxFontSizeMultiplier anywhere in frontend, fixed heights on chips (30 px), buttons (44) and the tab bar (60), and 26 numberOfLines truncations in 20 files; the a11y finder grepped for font scaling but reported nothing. app.json pins userInterfaceStyle light and nothing handles prefers-color-scheme, so a judge on a dark OS gets a white browser chrome and loading screen around a cream phone (F93 and F97 note the white frame but not the dark OS case). Nobody checked whether the Balanced preset or the ThemeProvider switch is reachable, nor whether the Figma Color collection modes (variables.md) match themes.ts beyond Expressive (F11 covers Expressive only). Browser zoom at 125 to 200 percent inside the 402x874 frame was never captured.
